Even when I'm around my family, it doesn't really fade. I might be sitting in the living room with them, but my mind is just... it's stuck on the warehouse. So, no, the tense feeling doesn't really go away. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Before the warehouse changed their hours and brought in the new inventory system, were you experiencing this kind of constant worry and tension, or did it really start after those changes happened?\n\nPatient: No, it wasn't like this before. Before they changed my hours and brought in that new system, I was doing fine. I was keeping my routine together, handling my responsibilities just fine. But... I'd say within the first few weeks after those changes happened, that's when I really started coming apart. So yeah, it definitely started right after they made those changes.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these changes happened a few weeks ago. How many days after the warehouse made those changes did the constant worry and tension start?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it wasn't right on the very first day, but... it was within those first few weeks. Before they made those changes, I was fine. I was keeping my routine together, handling all my responsibilities just fine. So, I can't give you an exact number of days, but it was definitely within those first couple of weeks.\n\nDoctor: When exactly did you first notice this constant worry and tension starting? Some mornings, I just sit there on the edge of the bed with my work boots on, just wondering if I can even face going into that place anymore. It's just gotten to be too much.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up in the morning, before all these work stressors hit you, how is your mood? So, I can't give you an exact number of days, but it was definitely within those first couple of weeks.\n\nDoctor: When exactly did you first notice this constant worry and tension starting? Was it within the first few days after the warehouse made those changes, or did it take a bit longer—maybe a week or more—before it really kicked in?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's hard to give you an exact day. It probably took a little bit longer than just a few days... maybe a week or so before it really kicked in and built up on me. But it was definitely within those first few weeks after the warehouse changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system. \n\nBefore they did all that, I was keeping my routine and my responsibilities together just fine. It was just sometime in those first few weeks after the changes that I really started coming apart.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned it took about a week or so before the worry and tension really kicked in. Like I said, I started coming apart within those first few weeks after they changed my hours and brought in that new system. Before all that happened, I was keeping my routine and my responsibilities together just fine. How long ago exactly did the warehouse make those changes to your hours and the inventory system?\n\nPatient: Well, the changes at the warehouse... they actually happened about five months ago. That's when they brought in the new schedule and the new inventory system. And, like I was saying, that's when all this worry and tension really started building up on me. It's been going on for about five months now.\n\nDoctor: Over these past five months since the warehouse made those changes, has the worry and tension been pretty much constant the whole time, or have there been periods where it got better or went away for a while?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not really sure if there were times it completely stopped. It feels like it's been pretty much constant. I mean, maybe there were a few days here and there where I was just too tired from the shifts to notice it as much, but... no, I wouldn't say it ever really got better or went away. It's just been hanging over me this whole five months.\n\nDoctor: So the warehouse made these changes about five months ago, and that's when this constant worry and tension started. Like I was saying a bit earlier, before they made those changes, I was doing just fine. I was keeping my routine and my responsibilities together without any trouble at all. I didn't have this kind of worry. It was really only within those first few weeks after they changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system that I just... started coming apart.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about five months ago after the warehouse changes. Like I said, I really can't pin down the exact day it started. But I do know that I started coming apart within the first few weeks after the warehouse changed my hours and brought in that new inventory system.
